  * **Citation**: :ref:`Caput VI, n. 7 & Caput VII, n. 3 (Gregorianum 1953, pp. 603–639) <caput-6-en-fn7>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: 'De connexionibus necessariis inter actus existentiales'.
    * *How it supports the text*: The extensive study that was directly incorporated with additions as the Appendix of this monograph.

----

.. _bib-vallee-poussin-charles-jean-de-la:

Vallée-Poussin, Charles-Jean de la (1866 – 1962)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Recherches analytiques sur la théorie des nombres premiers** (*Analytical Researches on the Theory of Prime Numbers*) 1896

  *Notes*: Landmark memoir proving the Prime Number Theorem; published in Annales de la Société scientifique de Bruxelles.

  * **Citation**: :ref:`Caput V, n. 8 <caput-5-en-fn8>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: G. H. Hardy's reference to de la Vallée-Poussin's proof of the Prime Number Theorem.
    * *How it supports the text*: Contrasts a long deductive proof (which compels belief through an intricate chain of syllogisms) with immediate intuitive arithmetic truths (such as 2 + 2 = 4).

----

.. _bib-hadamard-jacques:

Hadamard, Jacques (1865 – 1963)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **La géométrie (in Encyclopédie Française, Tome I)** (*Geometry (in French Encyclopedia, Vol. I: Mental Tools)*) 1937

  *Notes*: Survey essay on geometric intuition and displacement in Encyclopédie Française (Section I-52-10).

  * **Citation**: :ref:`Caput IV, n. 11 & n. 22 <caput-4-en-fn11>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: Hadamard's observation that geometric displacement and motion cannot be eradicated from spatial understanding.
    * *How it supports the text*: Confirms from a leading French mathematician that geometric congruence is fundamentally tied to spatial displacement.

----

.. _bib-russell-bertrand:
.. _bib-whitehead-alfred-north:

Russell, Bertrand (1872 – 1970)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **The Principles of Mathematics** (*The Principles of Mathematics*) 1903 (2nd ed. 1937)

  *Notes*: Russell's foundational logicist masterpiece (London: Allen & Unwin).

  * **Citation**: :ref:`Caput IV, n. 3, n. 4, n. 5 (nos. 390 ff., pp. 405–407) <caput-4-en-fn3>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: Russell's scathing critique of Euclid's superposition proof: 'It has no logical validity, and strikes every intelligent child as a juggle... to speak of motion implies that our triangles are not spatial but material.'
    * *How it supports the text*: Hoenen uses Russell's objection to make a crucial distinction: physical superposition of material bodies indeed cannot prove geometric equality, but mental translation of figures within intelligible matter (materia intelligibilis) is an authentic intellectual operation that grounds congruence.

  * **Citation**: :ref:`Caput IV, n. 28 (pp. 404 ff.) <caput-4-en-fn28>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: Russell's critique of empiricism: 'There is no evidence whatever that the circles which we are supposed to observe are true circles... geometry is not based upon observation of empirical shapes.'
    * *How it supports the text*: Enlists Russell's logicist critique to demolish sensory empiricism: mathematical figures are never found in physical sensation, confirming their status as abstract intelligible entities.

----

.. _bib-maier-anneliese:

Maier, Anneliese (1905 – 1971)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **An der Grenze von Scholastik und Naturwissenschaft** (*On the Border of Scholasticism and Natural Science*) 1943

  *Notes*: Groundbreaking historical study of late medieval natural philosophy and 14th-century Parisian and Oxonian physics (Rome: Edizioni di Storia e Letteratura).

  * **Citation**: :ref:`Appendix, n. 33 (pp. 312 ff.) <appendix-en-fn33>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: Maier's historical documentation of Nicole Oresme's doctrine of configurations of qualities and motions.
    * *How it supports the text*: Hoenen utilizes Maier's scholarly findings while offering a philosophical correction: Maier treated Oresme's doctrine primarily as a mathematical precursor to coordinate geometry, whereas Hoenen shows that it represents a profound metaphysical insight into the qualitative configuration of fluent existential acts (esse fluens).

----

.. _bib-ross-sir-william-david:

Ross, Sir William David (1877 – 1971)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Aristotle's Metaphysics, Physics, and Analytics** (*Oxford Critical Editions and Commentaries on Aristotle*) 1924, 1936, 1949

  *Notes*: The premier 20th-century critical Greek texts and English commentaries on Aristotle's works (Oxford: Clarendon Press).

  * **Citation**: :ref:`Caput I, n. 3 & Caput IV, n. 7 <caput-1-en-fn3>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: Ross's general monograph Aristotle (pp. 38–41, 54, 217) and critical edition of Physics IV.
    * *How it supports the text*: Standard scholarly authority for peripatetic abstraction and natural philosophy.

  * **Citation**: :ref:`Appendix, n. 3, n. 5, n. 13 (Metaphysics II, pp. 245, 264, 401) <appendix-en-fn3>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: Ross's recension and English translation of Metaphysics IX, 3 ('The word actuality, which we connect with complete reality...'), and his textual reading of ἔστιν (estin, with paroxytone accent indicating existence).
    * *How it supports the text*: Decisive philological support: reading ἔστιν confirms that Aristotle is treating actual existence in time rather than a mere copula, proving that the mover must already actually exist.

----

.. _bib-stammler-gerhard:

Stammler, Gerhard (1898 – 1977)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Begriff, Urteil, Schluss** (*Concept, Judgment, Inference*) 1928

  *Notes*: Epistemological investigation into the foundations of logic and judgment (Halle: Niemeyer).

  * **Citation**: :ref:`Caput V, n. 7 (pp. 229, 245) <caput-5-en-fn7>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: Stammler's demonstration that inference requires a synthetic apprehension of the relation between concepts.
    * *How it supports the text*: Confirms that logical inference cannot be reduced to mechanical algorithmic manipulation without intellectual insight.

----

.. _bib-gilson-etienne:

Gilson, Étienne (1884 – 1978)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **L'être et l'essence & Being and Some Philosophers** (*Being and Essence (1948) & Being and Some Philosophers (1949)*) 1948, 1949

  *Notes*: Gilson's landmark treatises establishing existential Thomism and distinguishing it from essentialism (Paris: Vrin / Toronto: PIMS).

  * **Citation**: :ref:`Appendix, § 7 and n. 23, n. 27 (L'être et l'essence, p. 248) <appendix-en-s7>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: Gilson's thesis opposing 'existential' and 'essential' metaphysics, and his claim that esse itself is not conceptualizable ('un centre d'obscurité qu'il nous faut traverser pour atteindre l'existence').
    * *How it supports the text*: Hoenen's pivotal critique: he praises Gilson for recovering the Thomistic primacy of esse, but demonstrates that Gilson goes too far in declaring esse completely unconceptualizable. Hoenen shows from St. Thomas that the intellect forms a true apprehensive concept of being (ratio essendi) following judgment, preserving the harmony between essence and existence.

----

.. _bib-hasse-helmut:

Hasse, Helmut (1898 – 1979)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Die Grundlagenkrisis der griechischen Mathematik** (*The Foundational Crisis of Greek Mathematics*) 1928

  *Notes*: Historical-mathematical paper co-authored with Heinrich Scholz in Kantstudien (Vol. 33, pp. 4–34).

  * **Citation**: :ref:`Caput I, n. 6 <caput-1-en-fn6>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: Hasse and Scholz's analysis of the crisis caused by the discovery of incommensurable magnitudes in Pythagorean geometry.
    * *How it supports the text*: Documents that the crisis of foundations in Greek geometry was resolved geometrically by Eudoxus rather than arithmetically.

----

.. _bib-lonergan-bernard-s-j:

Lonergan, Bernard, S.J. (1904 – 1984)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **The Concept of Verbum in the Writings of St. Thomas Aquinas** (*The Concept of Verbum in the Writings of St. Thomas Aquinas*) 1946–1947

  *Notes*: Five seminal articles in Theological Studies (Vols. 7 & 8); later published as Verbum: Word and Idea in Aquinas (Notre Dame, 1967).

  * **Citation**: :ref:`Appendix, § 7 and n. 24 (Theological Studies 1946, pp. 349–392; 1947, pp. 35–79, 404–444; esp. Vol. I, p. 353) <appendix-en-s7>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: Lonergan's demonstration that for St. Thomas, the term conceptio / conceptus is used not only for the definition produced in the first operation, but also for the interior word (verbum mentis) produced in judgment (the second operation).
    * *How it supports the text*: Provides decisive contemporary Thomistic backing for Hoenen's thesis against Gilson: St. Thomas explicitly recognizes that judgment produces an interior conception, proving that esse is conceptualizable in the second operation of the intellect.

----

.. _bib-freudenthal-hans:

Freudenthal, Hans (1905 – 1990)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **De fontibus geometriae** (*On the Sources of Geometry*) 1951

  *Notes*: Critical Latin article published in Gregorianum (Vol. 32, pp. 252–262) disputing Hoenen's scholastic philosophy of geometry.

  * **Citation**: :ref:`Praefatio, n. 1 & Caput V, n. 2 <caput-5-en-fn2>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: The debate between Freudenthal and Hoenen regarding whether axiomatic geometry can be constituted independently of intuitive abstraction.
    * *How it supports the text*: Serves as the vital contemporary sparring partner: Freudenthal defended modern formal axiomatics, prompting Hoenen to elaborate his 'semi-axiomatic' analysis and prove that axioms cannot function without semantic reference to intelligible matter.

----

.. _bib-alvarez-laso-jose-c-m-f:

Alvarez Laso, José, C.M.F. (1910 – 1993)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **La Filosofía de las Matemáticas en Santo Tomás** (*The Philosophy of Mathematics in Saint Thomas*) 1952

  *Notes*: Monograph on Thomistic philosophy of mathematics (Mexico: Editorial Jus, pp. 2–6).

  * **Citation**: :ref:`Caput VII, n. 1 <caput-7-en-fn1>`

    * *What Hoenen refers to*: Alvarez Laso's exposition of Aquinas's commentary on Posterior Analytics I, 1 regarding triangle as subject vs. attribute.
    * *How it supports the text*: Presents scholarly confirmation that St. Thomas's noetics of mathematics hinges on the intellectual activity that constructs figures in intelligible matter.

----

Summary Statistics
------------------

* **Total Authors Catalogued**: 64 authors spanning 24 centuries (c. 355 BC to 1993 AD).
* **Chronological Span**:

  * **Ancient Greek & Classical Foundations** (c. 355 BC – 560 AD): Eudoxus, Plato, Aristotle, Euclid, Alexander of Aphrodisias, Themistius, Proclus, Boethius, Simplicius.
  * **Medieval Scholastic Synthesis** (1274 – 1382 AD): St. Thomas Aquinas, St. Albert the Great, Nicole Oresme.
  * **Early Modern Epistemology** (1650 – 1804 AD): Descartes, Locke, Leibniz, Kant.
  * **19th-Century Mathematics, Logic, & Philology** (1864 – 1928 AD): Waitz, Möbius, Trendelenburg, Mill, Bonitz, Weierstrass, Hamelin, Poincaré, Couturat, Dedekind, Brentano, Cantor, Wellstein, Killing, Baeumker, Riehl, Klein, Hessenberg, Carteron, Burnet.
  * **20th-Century Axiomatics, Physics, & Neo-Scholasticism** (1930 – 1993 AD): Study, Pasch, Voss, Peano, Meyerson, Pirotta, Heath, Hausdorff, Hilbert, Enriques, Hardy, Geyser, Einstein, Weyl, Scholz, Amaldi, Hoenen, de la Vallée-Poussin, Hadamard, Russell, Maier, Ross, Stammler, Gilson, Hasse, Lonergan, Freudenthal, Alvarez Laso.

* **Footnote Census**: All 130 footnotes across the 9 sections of *De Noetica Geometriae* are cross-referenced to their respective author, work, and textual function.

.. _bib-additional-cited-authors:

Additional Cited Authors & Commentators
---------------------------------------

.. _bib-john-philoponus:

John Philoponus (c. 490 – c. 570 AD)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **In Aristotelis Analytica Posteriora & Physica Commentaria (*Commentaries on Aristotle's Posterior Analytics and Physics*) c. 520–530 AD**

  *Notes*: Alexandrian Neoplatonic and Christian commentator on Aristotle; edited by M. Wallies and H. Vitelli in Commentaria in Aristotelem Graeca (CAG Vols. XIII, XVI–XVII, Berlin: Reimer, 1887–1909).

  * *What Hoenen refers to*: Philoponus's commentary on Aristotle's Posterior Analytics and Physics regarding the nature of mathematical abstraction, spatial extension, and the pre-existence of moving causes.
  * *How it supports the text*: Provides late-antique Greek textual witness confirming the peripatetic understanding of mathematical abstraction and existential prerequisites of motion.

----

.. _bib-newton-sir-isaac:

Newton, Sir Isaac (1642 – 1727)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Philosophiae Naturalis Principia Mathematica & Tractatus de Quadratura Curvarum (*Mathematical Principles of Natural Philosophy & Treatise on the Quadrature of Curves*) 1687, 1704**

  *Notes*: Foundational treatises of classical mechanics and the method of fluxions, conceiving continuous geometric magnitudes as generated by continuous motion.

  * *What Hoenen refers to*: Newton's cinematic generation of lines and surfaces by the local motion of points and lines (fluxions), following the classical geometric tradition commented on by St. Thomas in In I De Caelo.
  * *How it supports the text*: Demonstrates that the generation of continuous figures through imagined motion in intelligible extension belongs to the perennial mainstream of mathematical thought.

----

.. _bib-hegel-georg-wilhelm-friedrich:

Hegel, Georg Wilhelm Friedrich (1770 – 1831)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Wissenschaft der Logik & Enzyklopädie der philosophischen Wissenschaften (*Science of Logic & Encyclopedia of the Philosophical Sciences*) 1812–1817**

  *Notes*: Foundational treatises of German absolute idealism analyzing quantity, the mathematical infinite, and the philosophy of nature.

  * *What Hoenen refers to*: Hegel's critique of the Cartesian Cogito and his dialectical treatment of quantity and mathematical demonstration.
  * *How it supports the text*: Contrasted by Hoenen with the realistic Aristotelian-Thomistic intuition of existential acts and continuous quantity.

----

.. _bib-gauss-carl-friedrich:

Gauss, Carl Friedrich (1777 – 1855)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Disquisitiones generales circa superficies curvas & Briefwechsel (*General Investigations of Curved Surfaces & Mathematical Correspondence*) 1827**

  *Notes*: Landmark differential-geometric memoir and foundational correspondence on non-Euclidean geometry and the concept of number.

  * *What Hoenen refers to*: Gauss's investigations into intrinsic surface curvature, non-Euclidean parallel theory, and the foundations of number and continuous magnitude (as analyzed by Gerhard Stammler).
  * *How it supports the text*: Marks the historical turning point toward non-Euclidean geometry and the modern critical examination of Euclid's Fifth Postulate.

----

.. _bib-helmholtz-hermann-von:

Helmholtz, Hermann von (1821 – 1894)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Über die Tatsachen, die der Geometrie zum Grunde liegen (*On the Facts Underlying Geometry*) 1868**

  *Notes*: Seminal memoir in Nachrichten von der Königlichen Gesellschaft der Wissenschaften zu Göttingen grounding Riemannian metrics in the free mobility of rigid bodies.

  * *What Hoenen refers to*: Helmholtz's analysis of congruence and spatial measurement through the transport and superposition of rigid bodies.
  * *How it supports the text*: Hoenen examines Helmholtz's empiricist appeal to physical rigid bodies to distinguish physical mechanics from the pure intellectual translation of figures in intelligible matter.

----

.. _bib-christ-wilhelm-von:

Christ, Wilhelm von (1831 – 1906)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Aristotelis Metaphysica (*Critical Teubner Edition of Aristotle's Metaphysics*) 1886 (rev. ed. 1895)**

  *Notes*: Standard critical Greek edition of Aristotle's Metaphysics published by B. G. Teubner (Leipzig).

  * *What Hoenen refers to*: Christ's critical apparatus and textual readings in Metaphysics V and IX alongside Bonitz and Ross.
  * *How it supports the text*: Provides philological verification of Aristotle's Greek text on actuality (energeia) and metaphysical diversity.

----

.. _bib-mercier-desire-joseph-cardinal:

Mercier, Désiré-Joseph, Cardinal (1851 – 1926)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Critériologie générale ou Théorie générale de la certitude (*General Criteriology or General Theory of Certitude*) 1899 (8th ed. 1923)**

  *Notes*: Foundational Louvain neo-scholastic treatise on epistemology and the criterion of truth (Louvain: Institut Supérieur de Philosophie).

  * *What Hoenen refers to*: Mercier's Louvain criteriology separating purely ideal judgments (connecting abstract concepts) from existential judgments of reality.
  * *How it supports the text*: Critiqued in the Appendix: Hoenen shows that necessary synthetic connections exist not only between abstract quidditative concepts, but directly between concrete existential acts.

----

.. _bib-holder-otto:

Hölder, Otto (1859 – 1937)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Die mathematische Methode & Anschauung und Denken in der Geometrie (*The Mathematical Method & Intuition and Thought in Geometry*) 1900, 1924**

  *Notes*: Influential epistemological studies on geometric intuition, axiomatic deduction, and measurement (Leipzig: Teubner).

  * *What Hoenen refers to*: Hölder's analysis of the interplay between spatial intuition (Anschauung) and deductive logical thought in geometric proofs.
  * *How it supports the text*: Supports Hoenen's thesis that deductive chains in geometry continuously rely on the structural intuition of spatial configurations.

----

.. _bib-brunschvicg-leon:

Brunschvicg, Léon (1869 – 1944)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Les étapes de la philosophie mathématique (*The Stages of Mathematical Philosophy*) 1912**

  *Notes*: Comprehensive historical and critical study of the philosophy of mathematics from Greek antiquity to modern logicism (Paris: Alcan).

  * *What Hoenen refers to*: Brunschvicg's historical interpretation of Aristotelian syllogistics, Cartesian analytic geometry, and modern mathematical idealism.
  * *How it supports the text*: Engaged by Hoenen to defend the authentic realist meaning of Aristotelian and Thomistic mathematical abstraction against idealist reconstructions.

----

.. _bib-taylor-alfred-edward:

Taylor, Alfred Edward (1869 – 1945)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **A Commentary on Plato's Timaeus & Plato: The Man and His Work (*Commentary on Plato's Timaeus & Plato*) 1928**

  *Notes*: Monumental British philological and philosophical studies on Plato and Pythagorean mathematics (Oxford: Clarendon Press).

  * *What Hoenen refers to*: Taylor's analysis of Platonic mathematical intermediates, Pythagorean geometry, and the divided line in the Republic.
  * *How it supports the text*: Clarifies the historical divergence between Platonic mathematical idealism and Aristotle's doctrine of abstraction from sensible phantasms.

----

.. _bib-reichenbach-hans:

Reichenbach, Hans (1891 – 1953)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Philosophie der Raum-Zeit-Lehre (*The Philosophy of Space and Time*) 1928**

  *Notes*: Logical empiricist treatise on the epistemology of geometry, coordinative definitions, and general relativity (Berlin: Walter de Gruyter).

  * *What Hoenen refers to*: Reichenbach's positivist critique of spatial intuition and his claim that geometric visualization is merely subjective and conventional.
  * *How it supports the text*: Refuted by Hoenen through the distinction between variable sensory images and the universal formal relations intuited by the intellect in intelligible matter.

----

.. _bib-becker-oskar:

Becker, Oskar (1889 – 1964)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Mathematische Existenz & Grundlagen der Mathematik in geschichtlicher Entwicklung (*Mathematical Existence & Foundations of Mathematics in Historical Development*) 1927, 1954**

  *Notes*: Phenomenological and historical investigations into Greek mathematics, Eudoxian proportion theory, and intuitionism (Halle: Niemeyer).

  * *What Hoenen refers to*: Becker's studies on the ontology of mathematical objects and the Greek theory of incommensurables.
  * *How it supports the text*: Corroborates the irreducible role of intuitive construction in both ancient Greek geometry and modern foundational research.

----

.. _bib-brouwer-luitzen-egbertus-jan:

Brouwer, L. E. J. (1881 – 1966)
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

* **Intuitionism and Formalism & Begründung der Mengenlehre unabhängig vom logischen Satz vom ausgeschlossenen Dritten (*Foundational Papers on Mathematical Intuitionism*) 1912, 1918**

  *Notes*: Seminal Dutch mathematical treatises founding intuitionistic mathematics in opposition to Hilbertian formalism.

  * *What Hoenen refers to*: Brouwer's insistence on primordial mathematical intuition over uninterpreted formalist symbol manipulation.
  * *How it supports the text*: Hoenen notes that while modern intuitionism rightly rejects empty formalism, it must be grounded in realistic Thomistic abstraction of continuous extension rather than Kantian subjective time-intuition.

----
